Options: (a) Vishwamitra (b) Agastya (c) Vashishtha (d) Sambhara
Answer: (b) Agastya
Explanation: Agastya is the sage who is credited with having Aryanised South India. It is believed that he travelled south on request of Gods and later resided there. He is also known as “Father of Tamil Literature.”

[13-081] Ch13 · South India (Chola, Chalukya, Pallava and Sangam Period) · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 2020
Q: Consider the following events in the history of India:
   1. Rise of Pratiharas under King Bhoja
   2. Establishment of Pallava power under
   Mahendravarman-I
   3. Establishment of Chola power by Parantaka - I
   4. Pala dynasty founded by Gopala
   What is the correct chronological order of the above events, starting from the earliest time?
Options: (a) 2 - 1 - 4 - 3 (b) 3 - 1 - 4 - 2 (c) 2 - 4 - 1 - 3 (d) 3 - 4 - 1 - 2
Answer: (c) 2 - 4 - 1 - 3
Explanation: Following is the sequence of events :- / Mahendravarman- I established Pallava Power (600-630A.D.); Gopala founded Pala dynasty in 750 A.D.; / Mihirabhoja (836-885 A.D.) or Bhoja I was a ruler of Gurjara Pratihara dynasty of India; / Parantaka - I (907-953 A.D.) ruled Chola kingdom in South India.

Options: (a) Karuvur/Karur (b) Kanchipuram (c) Madurai (d) More than one of the above
Answer: (a) Karuvur/Karur

[13-087] Ch13 · South India (Chola, Chalukya, Pallava and Sangam Period) · asked in 1 paper(s): Uttarakhand P.C.S. (Pre) 2010
Q: Which dynasty did not rule over North India?
Options: (a) Chalukyas (b) Rajputs (c) Gupta (d) Mauryas
Answer: (a) Chalukyas
Explanation: Chalukya Dynasty did not rule over North India. Their rule was mainly centralized in Deccan and Southern India. Their capital was Badami (Vatapi). Pulakesin - II was the first ruler of Badami’s Chalukya dynasty who defeated Harshavardhana in 618 AD. Apart from this, there were also small branches of Chalukyas in which the Chalukyas of Kalyani were most prominent.

[13-088] Ch13 · South India (Chola, Chalukya, Pallava and Sangam Period) · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.R.O./A.R.O. (Mains) 2016
Q: Which of the following King is known to have sent his ministers to suppress the cruelty against animals in Kashi region?
Options: (a) Chalukya King Siddharaja Jayasimha (b) Chalukya King Kumarapala (c) Chola King Kulottunga I (d) Kashmirian King Jayasimha
Answer: (b) Chalukya King Kumarapala
Explanation: Kumarpala was a Chalukya king. He reigned for 29 years. He was a Jain and a disciple of Jain Scholar Hemachandra. Several medieval Jain scholars wrote chronicles about him. As per Jain traditions he was against animal cruelty and sent his minister to suppress the cruelty against animals in Kashi region.

In the Pandya period, the temples were small, but many ramparts were built around their courtyard. [14-003] Ch14 · Ancient Literature and Litterateur · asked in 3 paper(s): 47th B.P.S.C. (Pre) 2005; U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 1992; M.P. P.C.S. (Pre) 2021

Options: (a) Aswaghosha (b) Vishakhadatta (c) Kalidasa (d) Bhasa
Answer: (b) Vishakhadatta
Also asked: Who was the author of Mudrarakshasa? -> Vishakhadatta
Explanation: Mudrarakshasa written by Vishakhadatta is a drama in Sanskrit. Although written during the Gupta period, it describes how Chandragupta Maurya with the assistance of Kautilya overthrew the Nandas. It also gives a picture of the socio-economic condition under the Mauryas.

[14-005] Ch14 · Ancient Literature and Litterateur · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Mains) 2009

   2. Kamasutra
   3. Meghaduta
   4. Mudrarakshasa
   Select the correct answer from the codes given below:
   Code:
Options: (a) 1 and 2 only (b) 2 and 3 only (c) 1, 2 and 3 only (d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
Answer: (d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
Explanation: Sanskrit literature reached its apogee in the Gupta period. This era is known for equal writing of prose and poetry. Amarakosh by Amarsimha, Kamasutra by Vatsyayana, Meghadutam by Kalidasa and Mudrarakshasa by Vishakhadatta are among the classic literature composed during this period.

   2. Raghuvamsam
   3. Shringar Shatak
   Select the correct answer from the code given below:
   Code:
Options: (a) 1 and 2 (b) Only 3 (c) 2 and 3 (d) Only 1
Answer: (b) Only 3

[14-019] Ch14 · Ancient Literature and Litterateur · asked in 1 paper(s): 67th B.P.S.C. (Pre) 2022
Q: Who wrote the book, Kiratarjuniya?
Options: (a) Bhatti (b) Shudraka (c) Kalidasa (d) Bharavi (e) None of the above/More than one of the above
Answer: (d) Bharavi
Explanation: Kiratarjuniyam is an epic poem by Bharavi, considered the most powerful poem in Sanskrit. It consists of cantos describing the combat between Arjuna and Lord Shiva (in the guise of a kirata, or "mountain-dwelling hunter") at Indrakeeladri Hills.

[14-023] Ch14 · Ancient Literature and Litterateur · asked in 1 paper(s): M.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 1993

Options: (a) Arthashastra (b) Politics (c) Medicine (d) Religion
Answer: (c) Medicine
Explanation: Charaka-Samhita is a comprehensive text on ancient Indian medicine known as Ayurveda. It was written by Acharya Charaka who is known as the father of Indian medicine.

[14-024] Ch14 · Ancient Literature and Litterateur · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2023
Q: Charak Samhita is divided into how many chapters and sections?
Options: (a) 60 chapters and 8 sections (b) 120 chapters and 8 sections (c) 100 chapters and 5 sections (d) 80 chapters and 7 sections
Answer: (b) 120 chapters and 8 sections
Explanation: Charaka is the renowned author of the oldest surviving text in Ayurveda, the Charaka Samhita. Its contents are divided into 120 chapters in eight sections.

[14-025] Ch14 · Ancient Literature and Litterateur · asked in 1 paper(s): Chhattisgarh P.C.S. (Pre) 2022

Answer: (d) Amarkosha
Explanation: Amarkosha was written by Amarsimha. Varahamihira has written Panchasiddhantika, Brihat Samhita and Brihat Jataka.

[14-027] Ch14 · Ancient Literature and Litterateur · asked in 1 paper(s): 69th B.P.S.C. (Pre) 2023
Q: Nitisara, an early book of politics, was written by

Answer: (b) Kamandaka
Explanation: Nitisara was written by Kamandaka. It refined the field of political thought in India and became one of the most influential works in the genre across South and Southeast Asia.

[14-038] Ch14 · Ancient Literature and Litterateur · asked in 2 paper(s): U.P.P.S.C. (GIC) 2010; U.P.P.C.S. (Mains) 2015
Q: The contemporary ruler of Kalhana, the author of Rajatarangini was –
Options: (a) Jai Singh (b) Harsha (c) Govindchandra (d) Jayachandra
Answer: (a) Jai Singh
Also asked: How many Tarangas are in Rajatarangini of Kalhana? -> Eight
Explanation: Jaisimha (1128-1149) was the contemporary ruler of Kalhana from Kashmir. Kalhana was the author of Rajatarangini, and he completed this pioneer creation during the reign of Jai Singh. Rajatarangini is a collection of 8 Taranga and 7826 verses. The conventional ancient history of Kashmir is narrated in the first three Tarangas.

[14-040] Ch14 · Ancient Literature and Litterateur · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2000
Q: Who among the following continued the Rajatarangini of Kalhana:
Options: (a) Bilhana and Merutunga (b) Bilhana and Mammata (c) Jonaraja and Merutunga (d) Jonaraja and Srivara
Answer: (d) Jonaraja and Srivara
Explanation: Rajatarangini, a metrical historical chronicle of the Kashmir particularly the kings of Kashmir, was written in Sanskrit by Kashmiri Brahmin Kalhana in the 12th century. Jonaraja continued the narration down to the reign of Sultan Zainul Abedin and called it, like Kalhana, Rajatarangini (Rajavali). After the death of Jonaraja, his pupil Srivara continued the history of Kashmir.

Explanation: Ashvaghosha, a noted poet, is considered to be instrumental in the propagation of Buddhism. He was also the writer of a famous court poem of his time called the Saundarananda Kavya.

[14-046] Ch14 · Ancient Literature and Litterateur · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre.) 2021

[14-048] Ch14 · Ancient Literature and Litterateur · asked in 1 paper(s): [I.A.S. (Pre)-2020]

   (3) Kalidasa is associated with Chandra Gupta-II
   Which of the above statements are right
Options: (a) Only 1 and 2 (b) Only 2 and 3 (c) Only 3 (d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (c) Only 3

[14-051] Ch14 · Ancient Literature and Litterateur · asked in 1 paper(s): [M.P.P.C.S. (Pre)-2019]
Q: Which among the following sources throw light on the history of Parmar Dynasty?
Options: (a) Navsahsank Charit of Padmagupta (b) Prabandha Chintamani of Merutunga (c) Udaipur Prashasti (d) All of the above
Answer: (d) All of the above
Explanation: The sources that shed light on the history of Parmar dynasty are Navsahsank Charit of Padmagupta, Prabandha Chintamani of Merutunga, Harsola copper plate inscription of Siyaka-II, Bhoj's record of Banswara and Betma, Udaipur Prashasti of Udayaditya period etc. In Navsahsank Charita, Mount Abu is described as origin place of Parmaras.

[14-058] Ch14 · Ancient Literature and Litterateur · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 2024
Q: Which one of the following is a work attributed to playwright Bhasa?
Options: (a) Kavyalankara (b) Natyashastra (c) Madhyama-vyayoga (d) Mahabhashya
Answer: (c) Madhyama-vyayoga
Explanation: The Sanskrit play Madhyama-vyayoga is attributed to Bhasa. Kavyalankara was written by Bhamaha. Natyashastra is a detailed treatise and handbook on dramatic art that deals with all aspects of classical Sanskrit theatre. It is traditionally attributed to Bharata Muni. Mahabhashya was written by Patanjali.

[14-067] Ch14 · Ancient Literature and Litterateur · asked in 3 paper(s): Uttarakhand P.C.S. (Pre) 2010; R.A.S./R.T.S. (Pre) 1992; M.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2013

[14-072] Ch14 · Ancient Literature and Litterateur · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre)-2021
Q: With reference to the history of ancient India, which of the following statements is/are correct?
   1. Mitakshara was the civil law for upper castes and Dayabhaga was the civil law for lower castes.
   2. In the Mitakshara system, the sons can claim right to the property during the lifetime of the father, whereas in the Dayabhaga system, it is only after the death of the father that the sons can claim right to the property.
   3. The Mitakshara system deals with the matters related to the property held by male members only of a family, whereas the Dayabhaga system deals with the matters related to the property held by both male and female members of a family.
   Select the correct answer using the … [shortened in this brief; the book continues]
Options: (a) 1 and 2 (b) 2 only (c) 1 and 3 (d) 3 only
Answer: (b) 2 only
Explanation: Mitakshara is a vivarti on the Yajnavalkya smriti, written by Vijnanesvara. Dayabhaga is a Hindu law treatise written by Jimutavahana. These treatises deal with the laws of inheritance. Hence statement (1) is wrong. In Mitakshara system, the sons can claim right to the property during the lifetime of the father, whereas in Dayabhaga system, it is only after the death of the father that the sons can claim right to … [shortened in this brief; the book continues]

[14-076] Ch14 · Ancient Literature and Litterateur · asked in 1 paper(s): Uttarakhand U.D.A./L.D.A. (Pre) 2007
Q: Aryabhatta was –
Options: (a) Indian Politician (b) Indian Mathematician and Astronomer (c) Indian Sanskrit Scholar and Poet (d) None of these
Answer: (b) Indian Mathematician and Astronomer
Explanation: Aryabhatta was a renowned mathematician and astronomer of ancient India. One of his major works was Aryabhatiya. The book deals with many topics like astronomy, spherical trigonometry, arithmetic, algebra and plane trigonometry. In the field of astronomy, Aryabhatta was the pioneer to infer that the earth is spherical and rotates on its axis around the sun. He is also considered to be the father of Trigonometry.

[15-040] Ch15 · Early-Medieval Period · asked in 1 paper(s): R.A.S./R.T.S. (Pre) 2018
Q: Which of the following rulers does not belong to Gurjara- Pratihara dynasty?
Options: (a) Nagabhatta-II (b) Mahendrapala-I (c) Devapala (d) Bharatrabhatta-I
Answer: (d) Bharatrabhatta-I
Explanation: The Gurjara-Pratihara dynasty, also known as the Pratihara dynasty was an imperial power during the last classical period on the Indian sub-continent, that ruled much of northern India from mid 8th to 11th century. They ruled first at Ujjain and later at Kannauj. The rulers of this dynasty were as follows - Nagabhatta I (730-760 A.D.), Vatsaraja (775-800 A.D.), Nagabhatta II (800-833 A.D.), Mihir Bhoja-I (836-885 … [shortened in this brief; the book continues]

[15-052] Ch15 · Early-Medieval Period · asked in 2 paper(s): U.P. Lower Sub. (Pre) 2002; U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2015

   | 1. Chola | 2. Pala |
   | 3. Gurjara | 4. Rashtrakuta |

Answer: (c) 2, 3, 4
Also asked: Who among the following was not a part of the tripartite struggle? -> Cholas
Explanation: After the death of Harsha, Kannauj was the center of attraction for various powerful dynasties. It was compared with Magadha during the Gupta period. In order to control the territory, Pala, Gurjara- Pratihara and Rashtrakuta started a tripartite struggle, which has been described as a most important event in the history of North India during the 8-9th century A.D. Finally, Pratiharas came out as the winner.

[15-057] Ch15 · Early-Medieval Period · asked in 1 paper(s): Uttarakhand P.C.S. (Pre) 2021

Options: (a) Dhruva (b) Govind-III (c) Indra-III (d) Krishna-III
Answer: (b) Govind-III
Explanation: Govind-III was the successor of Rashtrakuta king Dhruva. After consolidating his position in South India, he attacked North India. During his North India campaign, he attacked Gurjara-Pratihara ruler Nagabhatta-II and defeated him. Sanjan and Radhanpur copper plate inscriptions mention his victory.

[15-068] Ch15 · Early-Medieval Period · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 2016

Answer: (c) Waterwheel used for the irrigation of land
Explanation: Araghatta is a Persian wheel, which, is a mechanical water lifting device usually operated by animals like bullocks, buffaloes or camels, used in the irrigation of land. It is mentioned in the Panchatantra and Rajtarangini.

Explanation: In 1420A.D. brother of Ali Shah, Shahi Khan, sat on the throne of Kashmir with the name of Zain-ul-Abidin. He was greatest ruler of Kashmir who ruled for nearly 50 years. On account of his liberal religious policy, promotion of learning, and interest in art and architecture he was called 'Akbar of Kashmir'.

[16-003] Ch16 · Muslim Invasion of India · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 1995
Q: The word ‘Hindu’ as a reference to the people of Hind (India) was first used by:
Options: (a) The Greeks (b) The Romans (c) The Chinese (d) The Arabs
Answer: (d) The Arabs
Explanation: Most scholars feel that the name “Hindu” was pronounced by invaders who could not pronounce the name of the Indus River. As mentioned in Rigveda, Bharata is defined as ‘Sapta Sindhu.’ Iranians termed this country as Hindustan, and Greeks called it ‘India.’ About the Indian people, the word ‘Hindu’ was first used by Persians/Iranians.

[16-007] Ch16 · Muslim Invasion of India · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P. Lower Sub. (Pre) 2015

[16-008] Ch16 · Muslim Invasion of India · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 1992
Q: Muhammad-Bin-Qasim was a –
Options: (a) Turk (b) Mongol (c) Arab (d) Turk-Afghan
Answer: (c) Arab
Explanation: Muhammad-Bin-Qasim was an Arab ruler born around 695 A.D. He was the nephew and son-in-law of Iraq’s King Al Hajjaj. At the age of seventeen, he was sent by Caliph-al Walid to lead an army to Sindh (India).

[16-009] Ch16 · Muslim Invasion of India · asked in 1 paper(s): M.P.P.C.S. (Pre.) 2020
Q: According to Chachnama, what was the Capital of the Indus Country in the 6th and 7th century?
Options: (a) Deval (b) Arod (c) Lodawa (d) Barmer
Answer: (b) Arod
Explanation: Chachnama ("Story of Chach") is one of the main historical sources for the history of Sindh in the 7th to 8th century A.D, written in Arabic. It takes its name from Raja Chach of Sindh whose son Dahir stood against Muhammad Bin Qasim's invasion of Sindh in 8th Century A.D. Text states that Arod served as capital of the Indus country in the 6th and 7th century A.D. Hence option (b) is correct.

[16-010] Ch16 · Muslim Invasion of India · asked in 1 paper(s): 70th B.P.S.C. Pre-2024
Q: “Chachnama” was translated to Persian by
Options: (a) Nurrudin Muhammad Aufi (b) Shams-i-Siraj (c) Muhammad Ali Bin Abu Bakr Kufi (d) More than one of the above
Answer: (c) Muhammad Ali Bin Abu Bakr Kufi
Explanation: The Persian translation of Chachnama was done by Muhammad Ali bin Abu Bakr Kufi. Chachnama is a book written in the Arabic language, but there is no clear information available about the author of this book. Chachnama describes the history of Sindh from 680 to 718 A.D. The complete translation of this book from Persian to English was done in 1900 A.D. by Mirza Kalich Beg.

[16-011] Ch16 · Muslim Invasion of India · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P. Lower Sub. (Pre) 2015
Q: Who among the following was the founder of Ghaznavid dynasty?
Options: (a) Alptigin (b) Mahmud (c) Sebuktigin (d) Ismail
Answer: (a) Alptigin
Explanation: Towards the end of the ninth century, Trans-oxiana, Khurasan and parts of Iran were being ruled by the Samanids who were Iranian by descent. Among the Samanid governors there was a Turkish Slave, Alptigin, who, in course of time, established an independent kingdom with its capital at Ghazni and founded Ghaznavid dynasty, which soon took over the control of Samanid Kingdom.

[16-012] Ch16 · Muslim Invasion of India · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P. Lower Sub. (Spl) (Pre) 2004

   Select the correct answer from the given codes:
   Code:
Options: (a) Both (A) and (R) are true, and (R) is the correct explanation of (A). (b) Both (A) and (R) are true, but (R) is not the correct explanation of (A). (c) (A) is true, but (R) is false. (d) (A) is false, but (R) is true.
Answer: (c) (A) is true, but (R) is false.
Explanation: Turkish conqueror Mahmud of Ghazni succeeded his father in 998 A.D. He established a huge empire in Central Asia with it's capital at Ghazni (Afghanistan). For 17 times, he attacked India from 1000 to 1027 A.D. He was a ruthless invader and plunderer of wealth. In these invasions, his aim was not to establish any permanent Muslim rule in India but to take away the huge wealth of the country.

From the ninth century to the 13th century, the Chandelas ruled over Central India. Their first capital city was Khajuraho, which was later shifted to Mahoba.

[16-015] Ch16 · Muslim Invasion of India · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P. P.C.S. (Pre) 1991

Answer: (b) Utbi
Explanation: Mahmud of Ghazni wasjust a plunderer who mounted at least 17 raids in India. But in his kingdom in Afghanistan, he had shown love for art and culture. He patronized three persons: / i. Firdausi (Persian Poet, known as Homer of the East) who wrote Shahnama. / ii. Albiruni (a brilliant scholar from Central Asia) who wrote Tahqiq-I-Hind. / iii. Utbi (court historian), who wrote Kitab-ul-Yamni and Tarikh-i-Yamini.

[16-039] Ch16 · Muslim Invasion of India · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2018
Q: Given below are two statements, one is labeled as Assertion (A) and the other as Reason (R).
   Assertion (A): Turkish invasions on India were successful.
   Reason (R): There was no political unity in North India.
   Select the correct answer from the codes given below:
Options: (a) Both (A) and (R) are true, and (R) is correct explanation of (A). (b) Both (A) and (R) are true, but (R) is not the correct explanation of (A). (c) (A) is true, but (R) is false. (d) (A) is false, but (R) is true.
Answer: (a) Both (A) and (R) are true, and (R) is correct explanation of (A).
Explanation: There are many reasons for the success of Turkish invasions in India, however, the most prominent of them was the constant warfare among the Indian rulers. / India, at that time, was divided into small kingdoms. There were many small kingdoms in North and Western India (present-day Pakistan), and this made Turk's work easy. A strong kingdom in the north would have deterred Turks.

[17-001] Ch17 · Delhi Sultanate: Slave Dynasty · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P. P.C.S. (Pre) 1990
Q: Who was the founder of Slave dynasty?
Options: (a) Iltutmish (b) Alauddin Khalji (c) Balban (d) Qutbuddin Aybak
Answer: (d) Qutbuddin Aybak
Explanation: Slave dynasty was the first of five unrelated dynasties to rule India during Sultanate period from 1206 A.D. to 1290 A.D. Qutbuddin Aibak ascended the throne of Delhi Sultanate in 1206 AD and with him started a new line of successive kings in Delhi Sultanate. He was the founder of slave dynasty. Qutbuddin Aibak and all his successors were slaves to their respective predecessors.

[17-009] Ch17 · Delhi Sultanate: Slave Dynasty · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 2003
Q: How did Sultan Qutbuddin Aybak die?
Options: (a) He was treacherously stabbed to death by one of his ambitious nobles (b) He was killed in a battle with Taj-ud-din Yalduz the ruler of Ghazni who entered into a contest with him over the capture of Punjab (c) He sustained injuries while besieging the fortress of Kalinjar in Bundelkhand and succumbed to them later (d) He died after a fall from his horse while playing Chaugan
Answer: (d) He died after a fall from his horse while playing Chaugan
Explanation: In 1210 A.D., Qutbuddin Aybak died in an accident while he was playing Chaugan (polo). He fell from a horse and was severely injured. He was buried in Lahore near the Anarkali Bazaar. He was the founder of the Slave Dynasty in India.

Explanation: The two types of taxes implemented by Alauddin Khalji were- ‘Ghari Tax’ which was implemented on houses and huts and ‘Grazing Tax’ on milk-giving animals.

[18-028] Ch18 · Khalji Dynasty · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2014
Q: What was the boundary between the Delhi Sultan and Mongols during the period of Alauddin Khalji after 1306 A.D.?
Options: (a) Byas (b) Ravi (c) Indus (d) Sutlej
Answer: (b) Ravi
Explanation: After 1306 A.D., during the period of Alauddin Khalji, Ravi river was the border between Delhi Sultanate and Mangol. In 1306 A.D., under Kabak Mongols attacked but on the bank of Ravi river, Malik Kafur and Ghazi Malik stopped them. Hence, Ravi became the boundary between them.

[18-029] Ch18 · Khalji Dynasty · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 2022
Q: With reference to Indian history, consider the following statements:
   1. The first Mongol invasion of India happened during the reign of Jalal-ud-din Khalji.
   2. During the reign of Ala-ud-din Khalji, one Mongol assault marched up to Delhi and besieged the city.
   3. Muhammad-bin-Tughlaq temporarily lost portions of north-west of his kingdom to Mongols.
   Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
Options: (a) 1 and 2 (b) 2 only (c) 1 and 3 (d) 3 only
Answer: (b) 2 only
Explanation: First Mongol attack on India happened during the reign of Iltutmish. During the reign of Ala-ud-din Khalji, Qutlugh Khwaja (Mongol) marched up to Delhi and besieged the city. During that time Ala-ud-din Khalji was at Chittor advance. During Muhammad-bin-Tughlaq reign, Tarmashirin led a Mongol attack on India but Muhammad-bin-Tughluq failed the Mongol attacks by occupying Kalanaur and Peshawar.

Explanation: Alauddin Khalji took various steps to consolidate his empire. He set up a strong central government and raised a powerful and enormous army to protect his empire. Hence correct option is (d).

[19-003] Ch19 · Tughluq Dynasty · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Mains) 2017
Q: With reference to digging canals for enriching agriculture, who among the following is given the credit of being the first ruler of 14th century?
Options: (a) Balban (b) Iltutmish (c) Ghiyasuddin Tughluq (d) Raziyya Begum
Answer: (c) Ghiyasuddin Tughluq
Explanation: Ghiyasuddin Tughluq was the founder of Tughluq dynasty. He took a number of steps to improve the standard of farmers. He restored the old rights of Khuts and Muqaddams. The rate of land revenue was fixed at 1/10 or 1/11. He built canals for irrigation.

[19-004] Ch19 · Tughluq Dynasty · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P. Lower Sub. (Spl) (Pre) 2008
Q: The Sultans of which dynasty ruled the country for the longest period?
Options: (a) Khalji dynasty (b) Lodi dynasty (c) Slave dynasty (d) Tughluq dynasty
Answer: (d) Tughluq dynasty
Explanation: The Sultans of Slave dynasty ruled from 1206-1290 A.D. Khalji dynasty ruled from 1290 to 1320 A.D. The ruler of Lodi dynasty ruled from 1451 to 1526 A.D. The ruler of Tughluq dynasty ruled from 1320 to 1412 A.D. Thus, Tughluq dynasty ruled for the longest period.

Answer: (c) Muhammad-Bin-Tughluq
Explanation: Muhammad-Bin-Tughluq (1325-1351 AD) was the most learned ruler among all the Sultans of Delhi Sultanate. He was well versed in astronomy, mathematics and medical science and other genres.

[19-014] Ch19 · Tughluq Dynasty · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 2006
Q: Assertion (A): Muhammad-Bin-Tughluq issued a new gold coin which was called Dinar by Ibn Battuta.
   Reason (R): Muhammad-Bin-Tughluq wanted to issue token currency in gold coins to promote trade with West Asian and North African countries.
   Code:
Options: (a) Both ‘A’ and ‘R’ are individually true and ‘R’ is the correct explanation of ‘A’. (b) Both ‘A’ and ‘R’ are individually true but ‘R’ is not the correct explanation of ‘A’. (c) ‘A’ is true but ‘R’ is false. (d) ‘A’ is false but ‘R’ is true.
Answer: (c) ‘A’ is true but ‘R’ is false.
Explanation: Muhammad-Bin-Tughluq issued the new gold coins which were called ‘Dinar’ by Ibn Battuta. He issue the token currency in copper or Bronze coins to promote his military power not for trade with the West Asian and the North African countries in token currency of gold. Thus, Assertion (A) is true, but Reason (R) is false.

Also asked: Ibn Battuta visited India during the reign of: -> Muhammad-Bin-Tughluq

[19-022] Ch19 · Tughluq Dynasty · asked in 1 paper(s): M.P. P.C.S. (Pre) 1997
Q: On the death of which Muslim ruler, a historian said ‘the king was freed from his people and they from their king’?
Options: (a) Balban (b) Muhammad-Bin-Tughluq (c) Alauddin Khalji (d) Sher Shah
Answer: (b) Muhammad-Bin-Tughluq
Explanation: When Muhammad-Bin-Tughluq was in Daulatabad, a revolt took place in Gujarat led by Taghi. Sultan dashed to Gujarat to quell the revolt. Taghi was defeated and fled to Sindh. After restoring peace in Gujarat, Sultan moved to Sindh to kill Taghi. Sultan fell sick on the way and died on March 20, 1351 A.D., near Thatta. On his death, Badayuni wrote, 'the king was freed from his people and the people from their king'.

[19-027] Ch19 · Tughluq Dynasty · asked in 1 paper(s): 45th B.P.S.C. (Pre) 2001

Options: (a) Balban (b) Alauddin Khalji (c) Muhammad-Bin-Tughluq (d) Firuz Tughluq
Answer: (d) Firuz Tughluq
Explanation: Firuz Shah Tughluq was fond of slaves. The number of his slaves reached up to one lakh eighty thousand. He established a separate department (Diwan-i-Bandgan) to take care of them. He kept his full attention on their education. Each slave was paid 10 to 100 Tankas and sometimes, they also got estates. This hobby of Firuz proved detrimental to the state.

Explanation: Nasiruddin Mahmud Shah (1394-1412 A.D.) was the last ruler of Tughluq dynasty. During his reign, Khwaja Jahan founded the independent state of Jaunpur. Nusrat Shah, another son of Firuz Shah, had challenged Nasiruddin. As a result, Tughluq dynasty was divided into two parts. Both rulers ruled over Delhi at the same time. Nasiruddin was in Delhi and Nusrat Shah in Firozabad. The great Mongol commander Timur attacked … [shortened in this brief; the book continues]

[19-042] Ch19 · Tughluq Dynasty · asked in 2 paper(s): U.P. P.C.S. (Pre) 2002; M.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2005
Q: In whose reign did Timur invade India?
Options: (a) Alauddin Khalji (b) Bahlul Lodi (c) Firuz Tughluq (d) Muhammad-Bin-Tughluq
Answer: *
Also asked: In which year did Timur Lung attack India? -> 1398 A.D.
Explanation: The invasion of Timur on India took place in the year 1398 A.D. during the reign of Nasiruddin Mahmud Shah (1394- 1412 A.D.). Timur himself mentioned the purposes of invasion of India. Timur’s objective of attacking India was to fight and destroy the Kafirs and plunder wealth. Before leaving India, he appointed Khizr Khan as subedar of Multan, Depalpur and Lahore. Thus, all the given options are incorrect.

[19-044] Ch19 · Tughluq Dynasty · asked in 1 paper(s): 45th B.P.S.C. (Pre) 2001
Q: Which one of the following dynasties ruled over India after the invasion of Timur?
Options: (a) Lodi Dynasty (b) Sayyid dynasty (c) Tughluq dynasty (d) Khalji dynasty
Answer: (b) Sayyid dynasty
Explanation: The invasion of Timur (1398 A.D.) destroyed Delhi and started the fall of the Tughluq dynasty. Tughluq dynasty came to an end with the death of Nasiruddin Mahmud Shah in 1412 AD; after that, Daulat Khan became the new Sultan of Delhi in 1413 AD. However, Khizr Khan defeated him, who was the Subedar of Lahore, appointed by Timur. He founded Sayyid dynasty in 1414 AD.

[20-001] Ch20 · Lodi Dynasty · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Mains) 2016
Q: Who among the following Sultanate rulers was of Afghan origin?
Options: (a) Khalji (b) Tughluq (c) Sayyid (d) Lodi
Answer: (d) Lodi
Explanation: Lodi Dynasty (1451-1526 A.D.) was the last ruling family of the Delhi Sultanate of India. The dynasty was of Afghan Origin. Babur defeated Ibrahim Lodi in the First Battle of Panipat (1526 A.D.), which ended the Lodi dynasty.

[20-002] Ch20 · Lodi Dynasty · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 2006
Q: Which one of the following is the correct chronological order of the Afghan rulers to the throne of Delhi?
Options: (a) Sikandar Shah-Ibrahim Lodi-Bahlul Khan Lodi (b) Sikandar Shah-Bahlul Khan Lodi or Lodi Ibrahim. (c) Bahlul Khan Lodi or Lodi Sikandar Shah-Ibrahim Lodi. (d) Bahlul Khan Lodi or Ibrahim Lodi or Lodi Sikandar Shah.
Answer: (c) Bahlul Khan Lodi or Lodi Sikandar Shah-Ibrahim Lodi.
Explanation: Bahlul Lodi was the founder of Lodi dynasty. The correct chronological order of the Afghan rulers to the throne of Delhi is Bahlul Khan Lodi (1451-1489 A.D.), Sikandar Shah (1489-1517 A.D.), Ibrahim Lodi (1517-1526 A.D.). Hence, option (c) is the correct answer.

[20-010] Ch20 · Lodi Dynasty · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P. U.D.A./L.D.A. (Spl) (Pre) 2010
Q: Which one of the following Sultans is known for having abolished the tax on grains?
Options: (a) Alauddin Khalji (b) Ghiyasuddin Tughluq (c) Firuz Tughluq (d) Sikandar Lodi
Answer: (d) Sikandar Lodi
Explanation: The rule of Lodi dynasty persisted till the coronation of Sikandar Lodi. He took keen interest in land revenue management. He ordered his nobles to present the details of their expenditure and cases of embezzlement were severely punished. He introduced “Gaj-i-Sikandari for measurement of land which was later used by Sher Shah Suri and Akbar. Several grain levies were abolished by him.

Explanation: The Ghulam (Slave) dynasty or Mamluk rulers ruled during 1206-1290 AD. Khalji dynasty ruled during 1290-1320 AD. Sayyid dynasty during 1414-1451 AD and Lodi dynasty during 1451-1526 AD. So, option (d) is the right answer.

[21-003] Ch21 · Vijayanagara Empire · asked in 1 paper(s): M.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2022
Q: Vijayanagara Empire was established in which year?
Options: (a) 1336 A.D. (b) 1236 A.D. (c) 1436 A.D. (d) 1316 A.D.
Answer: (a) 1336 A.D.
Explanation: The Vijayanagara Empire was established in 1336 A.D. by Harihara Raya-I and his brother Bukka Raya I. They were warrior chieftains and loyal officials in the administration of Kakatiya dynasty, which ruled parts of the Deccan region. After the fall of the Kakatiya Kingdom to the Delhi Sultanate, the brothers were taken as prisoners but later released, and they eventually established their own kingdom.

Explanation: Sayana was a great commentator on Vedas. Two brothers Harihara and Bukka laid the foundation of Vijayanagara Empire, which was on the south bank of Tungabhadra river near Anegundi Fort. They were also known as Sangama brothers. It is said that a sage, Madhav Vidyaranya, and his brother Sayana were the inspirational sources of this empire.

[21-034] Ch21 · Vijayanagara Empire · asked in 4 paper(s): M.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 1997; 66th B.P.S.C. Re-Exam (Pre) 2020; U.P.P.C.S. (Mains) 2014; U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 1993
Q: The famous battle held in 1565 is –
Options: (a) 1st Battle of Panipat (b) Battle of Khanwa (c) IInd Battle of Panipat (d) Battle of Talikota
Answer: (d) Battle of Talikota

Also asked: The Battle of Talikota was fought in – -> 1565 A.D.
Explanation: The battle of Talikota (1565 A.D.), a watershed battle, was fought between the Vijayanagara Empire and the Deccan Sultanates of Bijapur, Bidar, Ahmadnagar, Golconda (Barar was not included). It resulted in the defeat of Vijayanagara and ended weakening one of the greatest Indian empires originating from southern India before the Maratha Empire. Talikota is situated in Karnataka near Vijayapura district.

[21-038] Ch21 · Vijayanagara Empire · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 2006
Q: When Raja Wodeyar founded the Kingdom of Mysore, who was the ruler of the Vijayanagara Empire?
Options: (a) Sadasiva (b) Tirumala (c) Ranga- II (d) Venkata- II
Answer: (d) Venkata- II
Explanation: Tirumala, the brother of Rama Raya, dethroned Sadasiva Raya and captured the power in 1570 AD. Thus, the rule of Aravidu dynasty started. After Tirumala, his eldest son Shri Ranga-I became king. Venkata-II became the king after Ranga-I, who made Chandragiri his capital. He was one among the greatest kings of Vijayanagara. He was a contemporary of king Wodeyar, who laid the foundation of Mysore State in 1612 A.D.

[21-040] Ch21 · Vijayanagara Empire · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 2016
Q: Regarding the taxation system of Krishna Deva, the ruler of Vijayanagara, consider the following statements:
   1. The tax rate on land was fixed depending on the quality of the land.
   2. Private owners of workshops paid an industry tax.
   Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
Options: (a) 1 only (b) 2 only (c) Both 1 and 2 (d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (c) Both 1 and 2
Explanation: Land revenue was the main source of income. The land was divided into four categories for purposes of assessment, namely wetland, dry lands, orchards and woods. The tax rate varied depending upon the quality of land, crop, soil, method of irrigation, etc. Thus, statement (1) is correct. The private owners of the workshop had to pay industry tax. Thus, statement (2) is also correct.

[22-004] Ch22 · Delhi Sultanate: Administration · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2019

Answer: (c) Revenue from Iqta was directly deposited in Sultan's account
Explanation: Iqta system in India was introduced by Iltutmish during Sultanate period. According to this system, the owner of Iqta (Iqtadar or Muqti) was given the responsibility of collecting revenue and taxes in his area. Revenue from Iqta was not directly deposited in Sultan's account, whereas it was used by the Muqti to meet his administrative and military expenditures. Muqti had to maintain troops out of this revenue … [shortened in this brief; the book continues]

[22-005] Ch22 · Delhi Sultanate: Administration · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P. P.C.S (Pre) 2025
Q: Given below are two statements one is labelled as Assertion (A) and the other as Reason (R). Assertion (A) The territories of the Sultanate could be broadly divided into two parts – the Khalsa and the Jagirs.
   Reason (R): The Jagirs comprised of the land under the control of the State.
   Select the correct answer from the code given below:
   Code:
Options: (a) Both (A) and (R) are true, but (R) is not the correct explanation of (A). (b) (A) is false, but (R) is true. (c) (A) is true, but (R) is false. (d) Both (A) and (R) are true and (R) is the correct explanation of (A).
Answer: (c) (A) is true, but (R) is false.
Explanation: In the revenue administration of the Delhi Sultanate, the standard division of territories is accepted as Khalisa and Jagirs (Iqta). Therefore, Assertion (A) is correct. / Reason (R) is incorrect, because land under the “direct control of the state” was considered Khalisa, whereas a Jagir was granted to a Mansabdar/Jagirdar as a revenue assignment, and it was not an area under the state’s direct control.

[22-007] Ch22 · Delhi Sultanate: Administration · asked in 1 paper(s): R.A.S./R.T.S. (Pre) 2018
Q: Who created the department of Diwan-i-Arz in Delhi Sultanate?
Options: (a) Balban (b) Iltutmish (c) Alauddin Khalji (d) Firuz Tughluq
Answer: (a) Balban
Explanation: Balban (1266-1286 A.D.) was undoubtedly one of the main architects of the Delhi Sultanate, particularly of its form of government and institutions. He separated Diwan-i-wizarat (Finance Department) and created a new Department Diwan-i-Arz (Military Department). He then reorganised the Diwan-i-Arz and deployed the army in different parts of the kingdom to put down the rebellion.

[22-009] Ch22 · Delhi Sultanate: Administration · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 2001

Answer: (a) Diwan-i-Bandagan - Firuz Shah Tughluq
Explanation: Diwan-i-Bandagan was established by Firuz Shah Tughluq. This department dealt with slaves. Muhammad-Bin-Tughluq created department ‘Diwan i-Amir-i- Kohi’ for agrarian development. Diwan-i-Arz (Military Department) was created by Balban.

[22-016] Ch22 · Delhi Sultanate: Administration · asked in 1 paper(s): 40th B.P.S.C. (Pre) 1995
Q: Which of the following indicates taxes levied on the land product?
   | i. Kharaj | ii. Khums |
   | iii. Ushr | iv. Muktai |
   Select your answer from the following codes –
Options: (a) only i (b) ii and iii (c) i, ii and iii (d) I, iii, and iv
Answer: (d) I, iii, and iv
Explanation: In Islamic traditions, Khums refers to the historically required religious delegation of the Muslim army to pay one-fifth of the spoils of war, the booty collected from non-believers after a military campaign.This tax was paid to the Caliph or Sultan, representing the state of Islam. Rest of the other are taxes levied on the land product.

[22-017] Ch22 · Delhi Sultanate: Administration · asked in 1 paper(s): U. P. P. C. S. (Pre) 2010
Q: Which medieval king of India introduced the ‘Iqta system’?
Options: (a) Iltutmish (b) Balban (c) Alauddin Khalji (d) None of these
Answer: (a) Iltutmish
Explanation: Iltutmish first introduced 'Iqta' system in Delhi Sultanate Period. In the Islamic empire of the Caliph, land was granted to army officials for limited periods in lieu of a regular wage. The Iqta system was introduced in the 9th century A.D. to relieve the state treasury when insufficient tax revenue and little booty from wars made it difficult for the government to pay salaries.

[22-022] Ch22 · Delhi Sultanate: Administration · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 1996
Q: ‘Sharb’ was a tax levied on:
Options: (a) Trade (b) Irrigation (c) Non-Muslims (d) Industry
Answer: (b) Irrigation
Explanation: Firuz Tughluq determined taxation according to Quran rules. He permitted four taxes approved by Quran: (1) Kharaj (2) Jizya (3) Khums (4) Zakat. He levied irrigation tax 'Haqq-e sharb' at 10% rate of the crop yield on recommendations of religious scholars. Firuz Tughluq abolished 21 grievous taxes of his time.

[22-030] Ch22 · Delhi Sultanate: Administration · asked in 1 paper(s): 39th B.P.S.C. (Pre) 1994
Q: Coins-Tanka, Shashgani and Jital of Sultanate period was made of which of the following metals-
Options: (a) Silver, Copper (b) Gold, Silver, Copper (c) Silver, Zink, Copper (d) Gold, Zink, Copper
Answer: (a) Silver, Copper
Explanation: Iltutmish was the first to issue regular currency and declare Delhi as the capital of his empire. He started the ‘Tanka’ and the ‘Jital’ coins, made up of silver and copper, respectively, in the Sultanate period. Shashgani was also a silver coin. The ratio of Tanka and Jital was 1:48.

[22-031] Ch22 · Delhi Sultanate: Administration · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2012
Q: On whose coins the name of the last Caliph of Baghdad appeared for the first time?
Options: (a) Qutbuddin Aybak (b) Iltutmish (c) Alauddin Khalji (d) Alauddin Masud Shah
Answer: (d) Alauddin Masud Shah
Explanation: On the coins of Alauddin Masud Shah (1242-1246 A.D.), the name of the last Caliph of Baghdad appeared for the first time. The last Caliph of Baghdad was Al-Mustasim. He held this position from 1242-58 A.D. The name of Caliph Mustanasir was inscribed on the coins of Iltutmish (served from 1226 to 1242 A.D.)

[23-001] Ch23 · Delhi Sultanate: Art and Architecture · asked in 1 paper(s): 63rd B.P.S.C. (Pre) 2017
Q: Use of paper started in India in -
Options: (a) 12 century CE (b) 13 century CE (c) 14 century CE (d) 15 century CE (e) None of the above/ More than one of the above
Answer: (e) None of the above/ More than one of the above
Explanation: Use of paper in India is considered to be started around 7th-8th century CE. As per available sources, written documents on paper were found around 11th century CE and during 12-13th century CE, paper was used in royal scriptures also. BPSC gave option (a) as the correct answer.

[23-002] Ch23 · Delhi Sultanate: Art and Architecture · asked in 1 paper(s): 42nd B.P.S.C. (Pre) 1997
Q: Which Sultan built Alai Darwaza?
Options: (a) Iltutmish (b) Balban (c) Alauddin Khalji (d) Firuz Tughluq
Answer: (c) Alauddin Khalji
Explanation: Alai Darwaza was built by Alauddin Khalji. It has a domed entrance which was constructed using red sandstone and is adorned with white marble. It was completed in 1311 AD by Turkish craftsmen. It is one of the first buildings in India in Indo-Islamic architectural style.

Answer: (b) Fatwa-i-Jahandari
Explanation: Ziauddin Barani was a Muslim political philosopher of the Delhi Sultanate. He wrote two famous works, "Tarikh-i Firuzshahi" and "Fatwa-i-Jahandari." "Tarikh-i-Firuzshahi" is a historical account of the Delhi Sultanate, and "Fatwa-i Jahandari" is a work in which Muslim rulers are guided to adhere to political ideals based on Islamic principles.

[24-024] Ch24 · Delhi Sultanate: Literature · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 1996

Answer: (c) Early forms of some of the modern Indian languages

The term “Apabhramsa” was used in medieval Sanskrit texts to denote early forms of some of the modern Indian Languages. Scholars have opined that different Hindi dialects of northern India and Kashmiri, Punjabi, Sindhi, Nepali, Shauryaseni, Marathi etc. are developed from Apabhramsa.

[24-035] Ch24 · Delhi Sultanate: Literature · asked in 1 paper(s): R.A.S./R.T.S.(Pre) 1999
Q: Which among the following musical instruments is the finest example of Hindu-Muslim harmony in musical instruments?
Options: (a) Veena (b) Dholak (c) Sarangi (d) Sitar
Answer: (d) Sitar
Explanation: Indian music motivated Muslim society to be passionate about music. Consequently, Amir Khusrau emerged as a great musician during Sultanate period. He invented ‘Sitar’ with a combination of Irani Tamboora and Indian Veena. He combined some Indian and Persian melodies elegantly and introduced some new melody styles like Iman, Zilf, Sajgari, etc.

[24-036] Ch24 · Delhi Sultanate: Literature · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2009
Q: ‘Tabla’ was introduced by:
Options: (a) Adil Shah (b) Amir Khusrau (c) Tansen (d) Baiju Bawara
Answer: (b) Amir Khusrau
Explanation: Amir Khusrau’s real name was Abul Hasan Yaminuddin Khusrau. He was a great scholar and poet of his time. He was the first author who used Hindi (Hindavi) phrases and words. The musical instruments like Tabla and Sitar were invented by Amir Khusrau in the 13th/14th century.

[25-007] Ch25 · Delhi Sultanate : Miscellaneous · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2014
Q: Who were called ‘Dastar-Bandan’?
Options: (a) Sufi Saint (b) Khan (c) Malik (d) Ulema
Answer: (d) Ulema
Explanation: During the Sultanate period, high-positioned or designated religious and judicial authorities honourable (Ulema) were collectively known as Dastar-Bandan, because they dressed with an official turban.

[25-008] Ch25 · Delhi Sultanate : Miscellaneous · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 2022
Q: With reference to Indian history, which of the following were known as "Kulah-Daran"?
Options: (a) Arab merchants (b) Qalandars (c) Persian calligraphists (d) Sayyids
Answer: (d) Sayyids
Explanation: In Islam, Sayyids, Pir etc., are an important groups of religious thinkers. Pir, Sayyids appointed as religious and legal officers, collectively were called Dastar-Bandan i.e. one who wears a cap or Kulah. Among them, Sayyids used to wear pointed caps and were known as "Kulah-Daran".

[25-021] Ch25 · Delhi Sultanate : Miscellaneous · asked in 1 paper(s): Uttarakhand U.D.A./L.D.A. (Pre) 2007
Q: Which of the following started during the Rajput period?
Options: (a) Sati Practice (b) Child-marriage (c) Johar Practice (d) None of these
Answer: (c) Johar Practice
Explanation: During the period of Hindu-Muslim conflict, Rajputs performed a distinct form of Sati known as Jauhar as a direct response to the onslaught they experienced. It was done because of Muslim invasions. Hindu women feared to become the “booty for the captor” and committed Jauhar and Sati to avoid rape, torture and other ignominies.

Options: (a) Wheat (b) Barley (c) Gram (d) Maize
Answer: (d) Maize

Explanation: The capital of the Hoysala Dynasty was Dwarasamudra, which is currently in Halebidu (Hassan district of Karnataka).

[26-035] Ch26 · Provincial Dynasty of North India and Deccan Province · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 1995
Q: Which one of the following monuments has a dome which is said to be one of the largest in the world?
Options: (a) Tomb of Sher Shah, Sasaram (b) Jama Masjid, Delhi (c) Tomb of Ghiyasuddin Tughluq, Delhi (d) Gol Gumbad, Bijapur
Answer: (d) Gol Gumbad, Bijapur
Explanation: The Mausoleum of Muhammad Adil Shah of Bijapur is known as 'Gol Gumbad'. It is one of the world's largest domes.

[26-036] Ch26 · Provincial Dynasty of North India and Deccan Province · asked in 1 paper(s): Uttarakhand P.C.S. (Pre) 2012
Q: Match the following monuments with their concerned ruler and select the correct codes given below:
   | A. Double Dome | i. Sher Shah |
   | B. Octagonal Tomb | ii. Muhammad Adil Shah |
   | C. True arch Tomb | iii. Balban |
   | D. Gol Gumbad | iv. Sikandar Lodi |
   Code:
   A B C D
Options: (a) iv iii i ii (b) iv i iii ii (c) i iii iv ii (d) ii iii i iv
Answer: (b) iv i iii ii
Explanation: | Monuments | Rulers | / | Double Dome | - | Sikandar Lodi | / | Octagonal Tomb | - | Sher Shah | / | True Arch Tomb | - | Balban | / | Gol Gumbad | - | Muhammad Adil Shah |

[26-037] Ch26 · Provincial Dynasty of North India and Deccan Province · asked in 2 paper(s): M.P. P.C.S. (Pre) 2017; M.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2019
Q: Which of the following rulers got the Jantar Mahal of Mandu constructed?
Options: (a) Mahmud Shah Khilji (b) Alauddin Khilji (c) Bhoj Parmar (d) Hoshang Shah
Answer: *
Also asked: Which ruler among the following constructed 'Jahaj Mahal' of Mandu? -> *
Explanation: 'Jahaj Mahal' in Mandu is a famous place which was built during the period of Ghiyasuddin Khalji. There is no Jantar Mahal in Mandu. Thats why this question was deleted by M.P. P.S.C.

[26-039] Ch26 · Provincial Dynasty of North India and Deccan Province · asked in 1 paper(s): M.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2010
Q: Who built Gujari Mahal?
Options: (a) Suraj Sen (b) Man Singh (c) Tej Karan (d) Akbar
Answer: (b) Man Singh
Explanation: Gujari Mahal was constructed by Raja Man Singh Tomar between 1486-1516 A.D. It is a unique piece of architecture.

[26-040] Ch26 · Provincial Dynasty of North India and Deccan Province · asked in 1 paper(s): M.P. P.C.S. (Pre) 2017
Q: Which of the following pairs is NOT correctly matched?
Options: (a) Adina Masjid – Mandu (b) Lal Darwaza Masjid – Jaunpur (c) Dakhil Darwaza – Gaour (d) Tin Darwaza – Ahmedabad
Answer: (a) Adina Masjid – Mandu
Explanation: Adina Masjid (Jami Masjid) situated in Pandua of West Bengal is a famous ruin which was built around 1364-1374 A.D. by ruler of Bengal Sikandarshah. Other options are correctly matched.

[26-041] Ch26 · Provincial Dynasty of North India and Deccan Province · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P. R.O./A.R.O. (Mains) 2021
Q: Match List-I with List-II and select the correct answer from the code given below the lists:
   | List-I | List-II |
   | (Monument) | (Builder) |
   | A. Atala Devi Masjid | 1. Nusrat Shah |
   | B. Chhota Sona Masjid | 2. Ibrahim Shah Sharqi |
   | C. Qadam Rasul | 3. Sikandar Shah |
   | D. Adina Masjid, Pandua | 4. Wali Muhammad |
   Code:
   A B C D
Options: (a) 1 2 3 4 (b) 2 4 1 3 (c) 2 1 3 4 (d) 1 3 4 2
Answer: (b) 2 4 1 3
Explanation: Correctly matched list is as follows– / | List-I | List-II | / | (Monument) | (Builder) | / | Atala Devi Masjid | – | Ibrahim Shah Sharqi | / | Chhota Sona Masjid | – | Wali Muhammad | / | Qadam Rasul | – | Nusrat Shah | / | Adina Masjid, Pandua | – | Sikandar Shah |
